# Grade 4 Maths — Chapter 11: Logical Reasoning 3 — Direction, Images and Figures

## Interactive Lesson Outline

### step_1: The Four Directions
- **tts**:
  - A compass has four main directions: North, East, South and West. Going clockwise they come in the order N, E, S, W.
  - On most maps North is at the top, so East is on the right, South at the bottom and West on the left.
- **on_screen**: A compass rose spins into place; the child taps each arm and hears its name.
- **check**: On a map with North at the top, which direction is at the bottom? — Answer: South

### step_2: Turning Left and Right
- **tts**:
  - A right turn is a quarter turn clockwise; a left turn is a quarter turn anticlockwise.
  - A half turn makes you face the opposite way, and four quarter turns bring you back to where you started.
- **on_screen**: A child figure on a compass turns; each quarter turn clicks the arrow one arm round.
- **check**: You face East and turn left. Which way do you face? — Answer: North

### step_3: In-between Directions
- **tts**:
  - Halfway between North and East is North-East. The four in-between directions are North-East, South-East, South-West and North-West.
  - To find the opposite of an in-between direction, change both parts: South-East becomes North-West.
- **on_screen**: Four thinner arms grow between the main arms of the compass, each labelled as it appears.
- **check**: Which direction is halfway between North and West? — Answer: North-West

### step_4: Walking on a Grid
- **tts**:
  - Follow the walk one part at a time. Moves North and South cancel each other, and so do moves East and West.
  - Along square roads, the shortest way back is the blocks you are away East or West plus the blocks North or South.
- **on_screen**: A dot walks along the roads of a town map, leaving a trail, and the blocks are counted aloud.
- **check**: Walk 2 blocks North, 3 blocks East, then 2 blocks South. Where are you from the start? — Answer: 3 blocks East

### step_5: Mirror Images
- **tts**:
  - An upright mirror swaps left and right but never top and bottom. A word in a mirror has its letters in reverse order, each flipped.
  - Water is like a mirror lying flat: it swaps top and bottom but keeps left and right.
- **on_screen**: A letter slides up to a dotted mirror line and its image appears on the other side, flipped.
- **check**: Which way does the curve of the letter C open in an upright mirror? — Answer: To the left

### step_6: Hidden Shapes
- **tts**:
  - A hidden shape must fit exactly on lines of the figure: the same size and the same way round.
  - Look for one special part first, such as a square corner or a slanted line, then check every side.
- **on_screen**: An orange cut-out slides over a busy figure and clicks into the place where it fits.
- **check**: Is a triangle hidden in a square that has one line from corner to corner? — Answer: Yes, two of them

### step_7: Counting Shapes Carefully
- **tts**:
  - Count by size: first the smallest pieces, then shapes made of two or more pieces, and last the whole figure.
  - Where two figures overlap, a shared part must be counted only once.
- **on_screen**: Pieces of a figure light up one size at a time while a counter adds them up.
- **check**: How many triangles are there in a triangle cut by one line from its top corner to the bottom side? — Answer: 3


## Practice Set A

### Q01
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-01
- **stem**: On Tejas's compass, only North is marked. Which direction does the arm with the pink dot point to?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 260 230" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="A compass rose with four arms; only the N arm is lettered and one arm has a pink dot">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="260" height="230" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="130.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Tejas's compass</text>
  <polygon class="part arm" points="130,54 118,124 142,124" fill="#ef5350" stroke="#455a64" stroke-width="1.4"/>
  <text class="label compass-letter" x="130" y="42" font-size="18" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#263238">N</text>
  <polygon class="part arm" points="200,124 130,112 130,136" fill="#cfd8dc" stroke="#455a64" stroke-width="1.4"/>
  <polygon class="part arm" points="130,194 142,124 118,124" fill="#cfd8dc" stroke="#455a64" stroke-width="1.4"/>
  <polygon class="part arm" points="60,124 130,136 130,112" fill="#cfd8dc" stroke="#455a64" stroke-width="1.4"/>
  <circle class="part marker" cx="82" cy="124" r="7" fill="#d81b60" stroke="#fff" stroke-width="1.5"/>
  <circle class="part hub" cx="130" cy="124" r="6" fill="#fff" stroke="#455a64" stroke-width="1.4"/>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: A compass rose with four arms. The top arm is red and lettered N. A pink dot sits on the arm on the left.
- **options**:
  - A) East
  - B) South
  - C) North-West
  - D) West
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: With North at the top, going clockwise the arms are North, East (right), South (bottom) and West (left). The pink dot is on the left arm, so it points West. East is tempting because East and West are easy to swap, but East is the arm on the right when North is at the top.
- **hint**: Start at the arm marked N and go clockwise round Tejas's compass, naming each arm as you pass it.
- **skill**: compass_arm_reading
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q02
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-02
- **stem**: At PT drill Ananya stands facing North. The teacher calls 'About turn!' and she makes a half turn on the spot. Where is she facing now?
- **options**:
  - A) East
  - B) North
  - C) South
  - D) West
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: A half turn is two quarter turns, so Ananya ends up facing the opposite way: North → South. East is tempting because it is one quarter turn from North, but a half turn needs two quarter turns.
- **hint**: A half turn makes Ananya face the opposite way. Which arm of a compass is opposite the arm she faces at the start?
- **skill**: half_turn_facing
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q03
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-03
- **stem**: At the bus stop, Harini looks East down the road. Her friend calls from her right, so she turns right to look. Which direction is she looking now?
- **options**:
  - A) South
  - B) North
  - C) West
  - D) East
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: A right turn is a quarter turn clockwise: North → East → South → West. From East, one step clockwise is South. North is tempting, but that is a left turn from East.
- **hint**: Turning right moves Harini one arm clockwise round the compass. Find East and move one arm on.
- **skill**: right_turn_from_east
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

- **alt**: Given: the letter F drawn with straight lines on a dot grid, with a dotted mirror line on its right. Figures 1, 2, 3 and 4 show the F turned or flipped in different ways.
- **options**:
  - A) Figure 1
  - B) Figure 2
  - C) Figure 3
  - D) Figure 4
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: An upright mirror swaps left and right but keeps the top at the top. So the upright line of the F moves to the right and its two arms point left, still at the top: that is Figure 1. Figure 3 is tempting because its arms also point left, but it is upside down, with the long arm at the bottom; a standing mirror never turns a picture upside down.
- **hint**: A standing mirror swaps left and right but keeps top and bottom. Check which way the arms of the F point, and where.
- **skill**: mirror_single_letter
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q05
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-05
- **stem**: Pranav draws a triangle and then one straight line from its top corner down to the bottom side. How many triangles are there in his figure?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 288 262" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="A triangle with one line from the top corner to the bottom side">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="288" height="262" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="144.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Pranav's triangle</text>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="220" x2="144" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="144" y1="40" x2="264" y2="220" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="220" x2="264" y2="220" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="144" y1="40" x2="114" y2="220" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: A large triangle. One straight line goes from the top corner down to the bottom side, a little left of the middle.
- **options**:
  - A) 2
  - B) 3
  - C) 4
  - D) 1
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: The line makes 2 small triangles, one on each side of it, and the whole big triangle is a third one: 2 + 1 = 3. 2 is tempting because you can see two pieces, but the big outer triangle counts too.
- **hint**: Count the small pieces Pranav's line makes first. Then ask whether the whole outline is a triangle as well.
- **skill**: count_triangles_one_line
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q06
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-06
- **stem**: Yuvraj is at the Temple on his town map. Which directions take him along the roads back to Home?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 360 300" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="A grid of roads with Home and four places marked, and a North arrow">
```

- **alt**: A grid of roads, 6 blocks across and 5 blocks up, with an arrow showing North at the top. Home is marked with a blue dot. The Temple, the Library, the Park and the Pond are marked with orange dots.
- **options**:
  - A) 3 blocks North, then 2 blocks East
  - B) 2 blocks South, then 3 blocks West
  - C) 3 blocks South, then 2 blocks East
  - D) 3 blocks South, then 2 blocks West
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: Home is 3 blocks below the Temple (South) and 2 blocks to its left (West). So he walks 3 blocks South, then 2 blocks West. 3 blocks North, then 2 blocks East is tempting because it is the way from Home to the Temple, but Yuvraj is going the other way, from the Temple to Home.
- **hint**: Put your finger on the Temple. Count the blocks down and across to Home, and name each move with its compass direction.
- **skill**: grid_route_instructions
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q07
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-07
- **stem**: Ritika writes four words in capital letters on cards. Which word looks exactly the same when its card is held up to a mirror?
- **options**:
  - A) MOM
  - B) NOON
  - C) DAD
  - D) POP
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: A mirror reverses the order of the letters and also flips each letter left to right. MOM reads the same backwards, and M and O look the same when flipped, so MOM is unchanged. NOON is tempting because it reads the same backwards, but each N flips into a back-to-front N in the mirror.
- **hint**: A mirror reverses the order of the letters and flips each one. Both changes must leave the word on the card unchanged.
- **skill**: mirror_word_same
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q08
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-08
- **stem**: The wind sock on the school roof points exactly halfway between South and West. Which direction is that?
- **options**:
  - A) South-West
  - B) North-West
  - C) South-East
  - D) North-East
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: The in-between direction takes its name from the two directions it lies between, with North or South said first: halfway between South and West is South-West. South-East is tempting because it also starts with South, but it lies between South and East.
- **hint**: An in-between direction is named after the two directions on either side of the wind sock, saying North or South first.
- **skill**: eight_point_between
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q09
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-09
- **stem**: The arrow on a weather vane points West. The wind swings it a quarter turn clockwise. Where does the arrow point now?
- **options**:
  - A) South
  - B) East
  - C) West
  - D) North
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: Clockwise, the directions go North → East → South → West → North. One quarter turn clockwise from West is North. South is tempting, but that is a quarter turn anticlockwise from West.
- **hint**: Clockwise means turning to the right. Move the weather vane arrow from West one arm to the right on a compass.
- **skill**: quarter_turn_clockwise
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q10
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-10
- **stem**: Vedant holds up a card with the word TOP in front of a mirror. Looking in the mirror, which letter does he see at the left end of the word?
- **options**:
  - A) T
  - B) a back-to-front P
  - C) O
  - D) an upside-down P
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: The mirror swaps left and right, so the last letter, P, appears at the left end, and it is flipped left to right: a back-to-front P. T is tempting because T is the first letter on the card, but in the mirror the first letter moves to the right end.
- **hint**: In a mirror, the right end of Vedant's word appears on the left. Which letter was at the right end of the card?
- **skill**: mirror_word_leftmost
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

- **alt**: Given: a boot shape on a dot grid, with the leg on the left, the toe pointing right along the bottom and a short slanted line at the top of the toe. A dotted mirror line stands on its right. Figures 1, 2, 3 and 4 show the boot moved in different ways.
- **options**:
  - A) Figure 1
  - B) Figure 2
  - C) Figure 3
  - D) Figure 4
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: In an upright mirror the boot keeps its leg at the top and its sole at the bottom, but left and right swap: the toe now points left, and the slanted line slopes the other way. That is Figure 3. Figure 2 is tempting because its toe still points right and looks neat, but it is flipped upside down, which is what a mirror lying flat (like water) would do.
- **hint**: Before choosing, decide which way the toe of the boot will point in the mirror, and whether the top stays at the top.
- **skill**: mirror_grid_drawing
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q12
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-12
- **stem**: A triangular tent flag is split into four parts by a pole down its middle and a ribbon across halfway up. Count every triangle, big or small. How many are there?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 288 262" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="A triangle with a line down the middle and a line across halfway up">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="288" height="262" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="144.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Tent flag</text>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="220" x2="144" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="144" y1="40" x2="264" y2="220" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="220" x2="264" y2="220" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="144" y1="40" x2="144" y2="220" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="84" y1="130" x2="204" y2="130" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: A large triangle. A straight line goes from the top corner down to the middle of the bottom side. Another line goes across the triangle halfway up, from one slanted side to the other.
- **options**:
  - A) 4
  - B) 5
  - C) 6
  - D) 8
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: Above the ribbon there are 2 small triangles and the small triangle made of both. Below it the pieces are not triangles, but each half of the big triangle is one, and so is the whole flag: 3 + 2 + 1 = 6. 4 is tempting if you count only the 2 small ones and the 2 halves, but the small top triangle made of both small ones, and the whole flag, count too.
- **hint**: Count the triangles above the ribbon first, then those that reach the bottom of the tent flag. Do not forget the biggest one.
- **skill**: count_triangles_cross_lines
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q13
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-13
- **stem**: Varun faces North. He turns right, turns right again and then turns left. Which way does he face now?
- **options**:
  - A) South
  - B) West
  - C) North
  - D) East
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: Right from North is East, right again is South, and then left from South is East. South is tempting because that is where Varun faces after the two right turns, but the last left turn moves him back to East.
- **hint**: Follow Varun's turns one at a time on a compass, writing down where he faces after each turn.
- **skill**: turn_sequence
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

- **alt**: A square hedge maze, 5 squares by 5 squares, with START in the middle square. Thick lines are hedges. There are four gaps in the outside hedge, labelled Gate 1, Gate 2, Gate 3 and Gate 4.
- **options**:
  - A) Gate 1
  - B) Gate 2
  - C) Gate 3
  - D) Gate 4
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: Trace every way out of START without crossing a thick line. Only the paths that lead to Gate 2 stay open; every other gate is shut off by hedges from START. Gate 1 is tempting because just one hedge separates it from the squares Nandini can reach, but she cannot walk through a hedge.
- **hint**: Trace with a finger from START, never crossing a thick hedge line, and see which gaps in the outside hedge you can reach.
- **skill**: maze_reachable_gate
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q15
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-15
- **stem**: In the park, Omkar walks 5 m North, then 3 m East, then 5 m South. How far from his starting point is he now, and in which direction?
- **options**:
  - A) 13 m East
  - B) 3 m East
  - C) 3 m West
  - D) 5 m North
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: The 5 m North and the 5 m South cancel out, so only the 3 m East is left: Omkar is 3 m East of where he started. 13 m East is tempting because 5 + 3 + 5 = 13 is how far he walked, but the question asks how far he is from the start.
- **hint**: Sketch Omkar's walk on paper. Which two parts of the walk undo each other?
- **skill**: walk_net_displacement
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

- **alt**: The orange shape is a thin right-angled triangle, 2 dots wide and 1 dot tall, with its square corner at the top left. Figures 1, 2, 3 and 4 are rectangles 4 dots wide and 2 dots tall with different lines inside.
- **options**:
  - A) Figure 1
  - B) Figure 2
  - C) Figure 3
  - D) Figure 4
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: In Figure 2 the orange triangle fits exactly: its long side along the top, its short side straight down, and the slanted line joining them, with the square corner at the top left. Figure 1 is tempting because it has the same triangle, but flipped, with the square corner on the right instead of the left.
- **hint**: Find where the square corner of the orange shape is, then look for a slanted line that fits with that corner in the same place.
- **skill**: embedded_shape_which_figure
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q17
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-17
- **stem**: A bar of chikki is marked into 5 equal squares in one row. Counting single squares and every longer block of squares side by side, how many rectangles can you find?
- **options**:
  - A) 5
  - B) 10
  - C) 15
  - D) 14
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: Blocks of 1 square: 5. Blocks of 2: 4. Blocks of 3: 3. Blocks of 4: 2. The whole bar: 1. Total 5 + 4 + 3 + 2 + 1 = 15 (a square is a rectangle too). 14 is tempting if you leave out the whole bar, but the whole bar is a rectangle as well.
- **hint**: Count blocks of each length of the chikki bar in turn, from single squares up to the whole bar, and add.
- **skill**: count_rectangles_strip
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q18
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-18
- **stem**: A shop's display shows a number made of light bars. Sahana looks at it in the upright mirror beside it. Which number does the mirror show?

- **alt**: A dark display with three digits made of green light bars: 2, 5 and 8. A dotted mirror line stands on its right.
- **options**:
  - A) 852
  - B) 258
  - C) 825
  - D) 528
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: The mirror reverses the order of the digits and flips each one left to right. Flipped, the 8 stays 8, the 5 turns into a 2 and the 2 turns into a 5. Reading the mirror from left to right: 8, 2, 5, so 825. 852 is tempting because it is the digits written backwards, but each digit also flips, and a flipped 5 looks like a 2.
- **hint**: Do two things: reverse the order of the digits on the display, then flip each light-bar digit left to right.
- **skill**: mirror_display_number
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q19
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-19
- **stem**: Damini walks North along a straight road. A temple is on her right-hand side. On which side of the road is the temple?
- **options**:
  - A) the West side
  - B) the East side
  - C) the North side
  - D) the South side
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: When you face North, your right hand points East and your left hand points West. So the temple is on the East side of the road. The West side is tempting because West is often drawn on the left, but that is Damini's left-hand side.
- **hint**: When Damini faces North, which compass direction does her right hand point to?
- **skill**: road_side_direction
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical


- **alt**: Given: the word FUN in straight-line capital letters, with a dotted water line under it. Figures 1, 2, 3 and 4 show the word flipped or turned in different ways.
- **options**:
  - A) Figure 1
  - B) Figure 2
  - C) Figure 3
  - D) Figure 4
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: Water acts like a mirror lying flat: top and bottom swap, but left and right stay. So the letters stay in the order F, U, N, each turned upside down: the F has its arms at the bottom and the U opens downwards. That is Figure 1. Figure 3 is tempting because its letters are upside down too, but it has also swapped left and right, so the word starts from the N; water does not swap left and right.
- **hint**: In water the top and bottom swap. Ask whether the F still begins the word, on the left, in the water image.
- **skill**: water_image_word
- **difficulty**: hard
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: achievers

### Q21
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-21
- **stem**: Shaurya draws a star by laying one big triangle pointing up over another pointing down. How many triangles of any size can you count in his star?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 288 322" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="Two big overlapping triangles, one pointing up and one pointing down, forming a star">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="288" height="322" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="144.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Shaurya's star</text>
  <line class="part seg" x1="144" y1="40" x2="24" y2="220" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="220" x2="264" y2="220" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="264" y1="220" x2="144" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="100" x2="264" y2="100" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="264" y1="100" x2="144" y2="280" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="144" y1="280" x2="24" y2="100" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: Two large triangles of the same size overlap to make a six-pointed star: one points up and the other points down. Their sides cross and make a six-sided shape in the middle.
- **options**:
  - A) 8
  - B) 6
  - C) 2
  - D) 7
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: Each of the six points of the star is a small triangle: 6. The two big triangles that were drawn are 2 more: 6 + 2 = 8. The six-sided middle is not a triangle. 6 is tempting if you count only the points of the star, but the two big triangles count as well.
- **hint**: Count the small triangles at the points of Shaurya's star, then add the big ones he drew to make it.
- **skill**: count_triangles_star
- **difficulty**: hard
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: achievers

### Q22
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-22
- **stem**: Ujjwal faces East. He walks 4 m, turns left and walks 3 m, turns left and walks 4 m, then turns right and walks 2 m. Where is he now from his starting point?
- **options**:
  - A) 1 m North
  - B) 5 m South
  - C) 13 m North
  - D) 5 m North
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: East 4 m; a left turn from East faces North: 3 m North; a left turn from North faces West: 4 m West, back above the start; a right turn from West faces North: 2 m more. He is 3 + 2 = 5 m North of the start. 1 m North is tempting if the right turn is taken from North instead of from West, so that Ujjwal walks back South, but he was facing West, and right from West is North.
- **hint**: Draw the walk step by step. After each turn, write which way Ujjwal faces before walking on.
- **skill**: relative_turn_walk
- **difficulty**: hard
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: achievers

### Q23
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-23
- **stem**: A ladoo tray has square cups in 3 rows of 4. Counting every square you can trace along its lines, small or big, how many are there?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 200 184" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="A grid of 3 rows and 4 columns of equal squares">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="200" height="184" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="92.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Ladoo tray</text>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="40" x2="160" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="74" x2="160" y2="74" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="108" x2="160" y2="108" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="142" x2="160" y2="142" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="40" x2="24" y2="142" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="58" y1="40" x2="58" y2="142" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="92" y1="40" x2="92" y2="142" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="126" y1="40" x2="126" y2="142" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="160" y1="40" x2="160" y2="142" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: A rectangle divided into 3 rows and 4 columns of equal small squares.
- **options**:
  - A) 12
  - B) 18
  - C) 17
  - D) 20
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: Small squares: 3 × 4 = 12. Squares 2 small squares wide: 2 rows × 3 columns of positions = 6. Squares 3 wide: 1 row × 2 positions = 2. A 4-wide square does not fit, because there are only 3 rows. Total 12 + 6 + 2 = 20. 18 is tempting if you count the small and the 2-wide squares only, but two 3-wide squares fit as well.
- **hint**: Count the small squares in the tray first, then the bigger squares in turn. Check how many of each size fit side by side.
- **skill**: count_squares_grid
- **difficulty**: hard
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: achievers

### Q24
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-a-24
- **stem**: Lekha faces South. She turns clockwise through three quarter turns, and then anticlockwise through a half turn. Which way does she face now?
- **options**:
  - A) East
  - B) North
  - C) West
  - D) South
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: Three quarter turns clockwise from South: West, North, East. Then a half turn (either way) faces the opposite way: from East to West. East is tempting because that is where Lekha faces after the clockwise turns, but the half turn still has to be made.
- **hint**: Do Lekha's clockwise turns one quarter at a time first. A half turn then makes her face the opposite way.
- **skill**: turn_arithmetic
- **difficulty**: hard
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: achievers

## Practice Set B

### Q01
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-01
- **stem**: On Bhumi's map of her village, North is at the top. Which direction is on the right-hand side of the map?
- **options**:
  - A) West
  - B) South
  - C) North-West
  - D) East
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: On a map with North at the top, South is at the bottom, East is on the right and West is on the left. West is tempting because it is easy to mix up East and West, but West is on the left of the map.
- **hint**: Picture a compass laid on Bhumi's map with North at the top, and read the arm that points to the right.
- **skill**: map_right_side
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q02
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-02
- **stem**: Early in the morning, Jatin stands facing the rising sun. Which direction is behind him?
- **options**:
  - A) West
  - B) East
  - C) North
  - D) South
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: The sun rises in the East, so Jatin faces East. Behind him is the opposite direction, West. East is tempting because the sun is in the East, but that is in front of Jatin, not behind him.
- **hint**: First decide which way Jatin faces when he looks at the rising sun. Behind him is the opposite way.
- **skill**: sunrise_behind
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q03
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-03
- **stem**: Pavan faces North. He turns right four times, a quarter turn each time. Which way does he face at the end?
- **options**:
  - A) North
  - B) West
  - C) South
  - D) East
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: Right turns from North: East, South, West, and the fourth brings him back to North. Four quarter turns make one full turn. West is tempting because it is where Pavan faces after three turns, but he turns a fourth time.
- **hint**: Each quarter turn moves Pavan one arm round the compass. Count the arms on a compass and compare with his turns.
- **skill**: full_turn
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

- **alt**: Given: the digit 4 drawn with straight lines on a dot grid, with a dotted mirror line on its right. Figures 1, 2, 3 and 4 show the digit turned or flipped in different ways.
- **options**:
  - A) Figure 1
  - B) Figure 2
  - C) Figure 3
  - D) Figure 4
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: An upright mirror swaps left and right only. The long upright line of the 4 moves to the left, the slanted line now goes down to the right, and the crossbar stays near the bottom: that is Figure 2. Figure 1 is tempting because it looks like a proper 4, but it is the drawing unchanged; a mirror always swaps left and right.
- **hint**: Look at the long upright line of Gargi's 4. After the mirror swaps left and right, which side will that line be on?
- **skill**: mirror_single_digit
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q05
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-05
- **stem**: Chetan and Elina stand back to back in the playground. Chetan faces South. Which way does Elina face?
- **options**:
  - A) South
  - B) East
  - C) West
  - D) North
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: Back to back means they face opposite ways. The opposite of South is North, so Elina faces North. South is tempting because it is Chetan's direction, but standing back to back, Elina faces the other way.
- **hint**: Standing back to back, Chetan and Elina look in opposite ways. Which direction is opposite to Chetan's?
- **skill**: back_to_back_facing
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q06
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-06
- **stem**: A toy robot starts at the purple arrow and follows the green route to its end. How many times does the robot turn right?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 360 300" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="A green route on a grid starting at a purple arrow that points up">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="360" height="300" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="150.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Robot route</text>
  <line class="part road" x1="30" y1="270" x2="30" y2="70" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="70" y1="270" x2="70" y2="70" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="110" y1="270" x2="110" y2="70" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="150" y1="270" x2="150" y2="70" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="190" y1="270" x2="190" y2="70" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="230" y1="270" x2="230" y2="70" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="270" y1="270" x2="270" y2="70" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="30" y1="270" x2="270" y2="270" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="30" y1="230" x2="270" y2="230" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="30" y1="190" x2="270" y2="190" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="30" y1="150" x2="270" y2="150" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="30" y1="110" x2="270" y2="110" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <line class="part road" x1="30" y1="70" x2="270" y2="70" stroke="#90a4ae" stroke-width="1.6"/>
  <polyline class="part route" points="30,270 30,190 110,190 110,230 190,230 190,110 230,110" fill="none" stroke="#43a047" stroke-width="4" stroke-linejoin="round" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <polygon class="part route-head" points="230,110 218,116 218,104" fill="#43a047"/>
  <polygon class="part robot" points="30,256 39,280 21,280" fill="#8e24aa" stroke="#fff" stroke-width="1.2"/>
  <line class="part north-arrow" x1="328" y1="86" x2="328" y2="60" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2"/>
  <polygon class="part north-head" points="328,54 322,66 334,66" fill="#263238"/>
  <text class="label compass-letter" x="328" y="102" font-size="14" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">N</text>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: A grid with a North arrow. A purple arrow at the bottom left points up. A green route goes up 2, right 2, down 1, right 2, up 3, right 1, ending in an arrowhead.
- **options**:
  - A) 5
  - B) 2
  - C) 4
  - D) 3
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: Ride along with the robot: up then right is a right turn; right then down is a right turn; down then right is a left turn; right then up is a left turn; up then right is a right turn. That makes 3 right turns. 5 is tempting because the robot turns 5 times in all, but 2 of those turns are to the left.
- **hint**: Pretend you are the robot, facing the way it moves. At each corner of the green route, decide: did you turn right or left?
- **skill**: route_count_right_turns
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q07
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-07
- **stem**: A boat sails South-East. Then it turns round to sail straight back the way it came. In which direction does it sail now?
- **options**:
  - A) South-West
  - B) North-East
  - C) North-West
  - D) North
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: Sailing straight back is a half turn, so both parts of the name change to their opposites: South → North and East → West, giving North-West. North-East is tempting because it changes South to North, but East must also change to West.
- **hint**: Turning to sail straight back gives the opposite direction. Change both parts of South-East to their opposites.
- **skill**: eight_point_opposite
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q08
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-08
- **stem**: Which shape can you NOT find anywhere in Ishita's drawing?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 224 170" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="A rectangle split into two squares, with one slanted line in the left square">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="224" height="170" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="112.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Ishita's drawing</text>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="40" x2="200" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="128" x2="200" y2="128" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="40" x2="24" y2="128" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="112" y1="40" x2="112" y2="128" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="200" y1="40" x2="200" y2="128" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="40" x2="112" y2="128" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: A rectangle twice as wide as it is tall, split by an upright line into two equal squares. In the left square, a slanted line goes from the top left corner to the bottom right corner of that square.
- **options**:
  - A) a triangle
  - B) a shape with 5 sides
  - C) a square
  - D) a rectangle that is not a square
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: The slanted line cuts the left square into two triangles, the two halves are squares, and the whole drawing is a rectangle that is not a square. No group of lines closes up into a shape with exactly 5 sides and 5 corners. A rectangle that is not a square is tempting if you only look at the small pieces, but the whole outline is one.
- **hint**: Look at small pieces and at bigger shapes made of several pieces of Ishita's drawing. Check the whole outline too.
- **skill**: shape_not_in_figure
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q09
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-09
- **stem**: Faizal's house number is painted on a tile. Which of these numbers would read exactly as it is when seen in a mirror?
- **options**:
  - A) 806
  - B) 808
  - C) 380
  - D) 618
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: A mirror reverses the order of the digits and flips each digit. 8 and 0 look the same when flipped, and 808 reads the same backwards, so 808 is unchanged. 806 is tempting because it starts with 8 and 0, but the 6 flips into a back-to-front 6 and moves to the front.
- **hint**: Faizal's mirror reverses the order and flips each digit. Which digits look the same when flipped, and which number reads the same backwards?
- **skill**: mirror_number_same
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q10
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-10
- **stem**: Keerthi faces South at the school gate. Her friend stands on her left. In which direction is the friend from Keerthi?
- **options**:
  - A) West
  - B) North
  - C) South
  - D) East
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: Facing South, Keerthi's right hand points West and her left hand points East, so the friend is to the East. West is tempting because West is on the left of a map, but Keerthi faces South, so her left is East.
- **hint**: Stand up and face the way Keerthi faces on a compass. Which direction does your left hand point to?
- **skill**: left_side_when_facing
- **difficulty**: easy
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

- **alt**: Given: the letters P and R in straight-line capitals on a dot grid, with a dotted mirror line on their right. Figures 1, 2, 3 and 4 show the two letters flipped or swapped in different ways.
- **options**:
  - A) Figure 1
  - B) Figure 2
  - C) Figure 3
  - D) Figure 4
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: A mirror does two things at once: the R, nearer the mirror, comes first, and each letter is flipped left to right, so both bowls face left. That is Figure 3. Figure 1 is tempting because both letters are flipped, but the P is still first; the mirror also swaps the order of the letters.
- **hint**: Two things change in a mirror: the order of Bhuvan's letters and the way each letter faces. Check both in every figure.
- **skill**: mirror_two_letters
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q12
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-12
- **stem**: A square tile has one line from corner to corner and two lines through its middle, one across and one up and down. How many triangles are there on the tile?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 208 242" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="A square with one corner-to-corner line and two middle lines">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="208" height="242" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="104.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Square tile</text>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="40" x2="184" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="184" y1="40" x2="184" y2="200" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="184" y1="200" x2="24" y2="200" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="200" x2="24" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="40" x2="184" y2="200" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="104" y1="40" x2="104" y2="200" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="120" x2="184" y2="120" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: A square. One straight line joins the top left corner to the bottom right corner. One line crosses the middle from left to right and another from top to bottom.
- **options**:
  - A) 6
  - B) 4
  - C) 8
  - D) 5
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: The middle lines make four small squares, and the slanted line cuts two of them (top left and bottom right) into 2 small triangles each: 4 small triangles. The slanted line also cuts the whole tile into 2 big triangles. Total 4 + 2 = 6. 4 is tempting if you count only the small triangles, but the two big halves of the tile are triangles too.
- **hint**: Count the small triangles on the tile first. Then look for big triangles that use the whole corner-to-corner line as a side.
- **skill**: count_triangles_square_lines
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q13
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-13
- **stem**: Mehul and Anvi start back to back at the gate. Mehul walks 6 m North and Anvi walks 4 m South. How far apart are they now?
- **options**:
  - A) 2 m
  - B) 10 m
  - C) 6 m
  - D) 24 m
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: They walk in opposite directions from the same gate, so their distances add up: 6 + 4 = 10 m. 2 m is tempting (6 − 4), but that would be right only if they had walked the same way.
- **hint**: Draw the gate as a dot, with Mehul's walk going up and Anvi's going down. Are both walks on the same side of the gate?
- **skill**: opposite_walks_distance
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

- **options**:
  - A) 14 steps
  - B) 8 steps
  - C) 12 steps
  - D) 11 steps
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: Following the open paths, the quickest way needs 12 steps; every other way round the hedges is longer. 8 steps is tempting because END is 4 squares up and 4 squares across from START, but the hedges block the straight way, so the route has to bend round them.
- **hint**: Write a step number in each square, moving out from START: 1 for its open neighbours, then 2, and so on up to END.
- **skill**: maze_shortest_steps
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q15
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-15
- **stem**: In Lekhapur, the temple is East of the school, and the school is North of the bank. Standing at the bank, in which direction is the temple, roughly?
- **options**:
  - A) South-West
  - B) North-West
  - C) South-East
  - D) North-East
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: From the bank, go North to reach the school, then East to reach the temple. So the temple is up and to the right of the bank: North-East. South-West is tempting because it uses the same two clues, but it is the direction of the bank seen from the temple, the wrong way round.
- **hint**: Draw the bank as a dot, then place the school and the temple using the two clues. Which way is the temple from the bank?
- **skill**: relative_position_chain
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q16
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-16
- **stem**: The orange shape is a square with one slanted line from its top left corner to its bottom right corner. How many copies of it, the same size and the same way round, can you find in Mrinal's drawing?

- **alt**: The orange shape: one small square with a slanted line from its top left corner to its bottom right corner. The drawing: 2 rows of 4 small squares. Top row, from the left: a line from top left to bottom right; both slanted lines (a cross); a line from top right to bottom left; a line from top left to bottom right. Bottom row, from the left: no line; a line from top left to bottom right; both slanted lines (a cross); a line from top right to bottom left.
- **options**:
  - A) 3
  - B) 5
  - C) 7
  - D) 4
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: Squares with just the top-left-to-bottom-right line: 3. Each square with a cross also contains that line, so it hides a copy too: 2 more. Total 3 + 2 = 5. 3 is tempting if you skip the crossed squares, but a cross includes the slanted line you are looking for.
- **hint**: Check each small square of Mrinal's drawing. Does it contain a slanted line going the same way, even if another line is there too?
- **skill**: count_hidden_copies
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q17
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-17
- **stem**: A shelf has 4 equal boxes in a row. The first two boxes from the left are split in half by a line across. How many rectangles of any size are in the drawing?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 208 162" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="A row of four equal boxes; the two on the left are split by a line across their middle">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="208" height="162" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="104.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Shelf</text>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="40" x2="184" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="120" x2="184" y2="120" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="40" x2="24" y2="120" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="64" y1="40" x2="64" y2="120" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="104" y1="40" x2="104" y2="120" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="144" y1="40" x2="144" y2="120" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="184" y1="40" x2="184" y2="120" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="80" x2="104" y2="80" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: A long rectangle divided by upright lines into 4 equal boxes. A line across the middle runs through the 2 boxes on the left only.
- **options**:
  - A) 10
  - B) 14
  - C) 16
  - D) 12
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: Using full-height boxes: 4 + 3 + 2 + 1 = 10 rectangles. The line across makes a top row and a bottom row in the two left boxes, and each row has 2 + 1 = 3 rectangles. Total 10 + 3 + 3 = 16. 10 is tempting if you forget the half-height rectangles made by the line across.
- **hint**: First count rectangles that use the full height of the shelf. Then count the shorter ones made by the line across.
- **skill**: count_rectangles_partial_line
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q18
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-18
- **stem**: Navya walks 4 blocks East, then 2 blocks North, then 1 block West along the roads of her colony, where the roads form square blocks. What is the shortest walk back to her start along the roads?
- **options**:
  - A) 5 blocks
  - B) 7 blocks
  - C) 3 blocks
  - D) 6 blocks
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: After the walk Navya is 4 − 1 = 3 blocks East and 2 blocks North of the start. Along the roads she must cover 3 blocks West and 2 blocks South: 3 + 2 = 5 blocks. 7 blocks is tempting because 4 + 2 + 1 = 7 is how far she walked, but going back the same way is not the shortest route.
- **hint**: Work out how many blocks East and how many blocks North of the start Navya ends up, then walk straight back along the roads.
- **skill**: shortest_way_back_blocks
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

### Q19
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-19
- **stem**: In the evening, Advait walks straight towards his own shadow. In which direction is he walking?
- **options**:
  - A) West
  - B) North
  - C) East
  - D) South
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: In the evening the sun is in the West, and a shadow falls on the side away from the sun, to the East. Walking towards his shadow, Advait walks East. West is tempting because the evening sun is in the West, but that is behind Advait.
- **hint**: Where is the sun in the evening? A shadow points away from the sun, so decide where Advait's shadow lies.
- **skill**: shadow_evening
- **difficulty**: medium
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: logical

- **alt**: Given: a flag on a pole drawn on a dot grid. The pole is on the left with a short foot at its bottom, and the flag is at the top, flying to the right with a notch in its end. A dotted water line lies under it. Figures 1, 2, 3 and 4 show the flag flipped or turned in different ways.
- **options**:
  - A) Figure 1
  - B) Figure 2
  - C) Figure 3
  - D) Figure 4
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: Water is a mirror lying flat: top and bottom swap but left and right stay. So the pole stays on the left with its foot now at the top, and the flag hangs at the bottom, still flying to the right: Figure 1. Figure 3 is tempting because it is also upside down, but it is turned right round, so the flag flies to the left; water never swaps left and right.
- **hint**: In water, does the flag still fly to the same side? Decide that, then check where the flag sits on the pole.
- **skill**: mirror_horizontal_drawing
- **difficulty**: hard
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: achievers

### Q21
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-21
- **stem**: A big triangle is cut by lines into 9 small triangles of the same size, as shown. How many triangles of any size are there altogether?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 264 298" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="A big triangle divided into 9 small equal triangles">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="264" height="298" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="132.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Triangle grid</text>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="256" x2="240" y2="256" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="24" y1="256" x2="132" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="240" y1="256" x2="132" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="60" y1="184" x2="204" y2="184" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="96" y1="112" x2="168" y2="112" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="96" y1="256" x2="168" y2="112" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="168" y1="256" x2="204" y2="184" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="96" y1="256" x2="60" y2="184" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="168" y1="256" x2="96" y2="112" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: A big triangle whose sides are each cut into 3 equal parts. Lines parallel to the sides join these points, making 9 small triangles: 6 pointing up and 3 pointing down.
- **options**:
  - A) 13
  - B) 9
  - C) 10
  - D) 11
- **answer**: A
- **explanation**: Small triangles: 9 (6 pointing up and 3 pointing down). Triangles made of 4 small ones, 2 small sides tall: 3, all pointing up. The whole triangle: 1. Total 9 + 3 + 1 = 13. 10 is tempting if you add only the big triangle to the small ones, but three medium triangles fit inside too.
- **hint**: Count by size: the small triangles first, then triangles whose sides are two small sides long, then the biggest one.
- **skill**: count_triangles_triangle_grid
- **difficulty**: hard
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: achievers

### Q22
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-22
- **stem**: Reyansh's robot starts in the square with the purple arrow, facing the way the arrow points. Its card says: forward 2 squares, turn right, forward 3 squares, turn left, forward 1 square. Where does it stop?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg

  <circle class="part place" cx="170" cy="210" r="7" fill="#fb8c00" stroke="#fff" stroke-width="1.5"/>
  <text class="label place-name" x="170" y="199" font-size="12" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#263238">Hoop</text>
  <polygon class="part robot" points="50,236 59,260 41,260" fill="#8e24aa" stroke="#fff" stroke-width="1.2"/>
  <line class="part north-arrow" x1="288" y1="86" x2="288" y2="60" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2"/>
```

- **alt**: A grid of 5 by 5 squares with a North arrow. A purple robot arrow in the bottom left square points up. Four squares hold toys marked with orange dots: a Flag, a Ball, a Cone and a Hoop.
- **options**:
  - A) at the Ball
  - B) at the Flag
  - C) at the Cone
  - D) at the Hoop
- **answer**: B
- **explanation**: Up 2 squares; a right turn from facing North means facing East: 3 squares right; a left turn from East means facing North again: 1 square up. The robot ends 3 squares right and 3 squares up from its start, on the Flag. At the Hoop is tempting if the last turn is made to the right, but the card says turn left, so the robot goes up, not down.
- **hint**: Move a finger square by square, and after each turn on the card say which way the robot now faces before moving.
- **skill**: robot_instruction_card
- **difficulty**: hard
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: achievers

### Q23
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-23
- **stem**: Two square rangoli frames, each made of 4 small squares, overlap so that they share exactly one small square, as shown. How many squares of any size are in the whole drawing?

**Diagram (SVG):**

```svg
<svg viewBox="0 0 264 232" xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" font-family="Arial, Helvetica, sans-serif" role="img" aria-label="Two 2 by 2 square grids overlapping in one small square">
  <rect class="bg" x="0" y="0" width="264" height="232" fill="#fff"/>
  <text class="title" x="132.0" y="22" font-size="15" text-anchor="middle" font-weight="bold" fill="#333">Overlapping rangoli frames</text>
  <line class="part seg" x1="57" y1="40" x2="157" y2="40" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="57" y1="90" x2="157" y2="90" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="57" y1="140" x2="157" y2="140" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="57" y1="40" x2="57" y2="140" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="107" y1="40" x2="107" y2="140" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="157" y1="40" x2="157" y2="140" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="107" y1="90" x2="207" y2="90" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="107" y1="140" x2="207" y2="140" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="107" y1="190" x2="207" y2="190" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="107" y1="90" x2="107" y2="190" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="157" y1="90" x2="157" y2="190" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
  <line class="part seg" x1="207" y1="90" x2="207" y2="190" stroke="#263238" stroke-width="2.4" stroke-linecap="round"/>
</svg>
```

- **alt**: Two square frames, each split into 2 by 2 small squares. The second frame is moved one small square down and to the right, so the two frames share one small square in the middle.
- **options**:
  - A) 10
  - B) 8
  - C) 11
  - D) 9
- **answer**: D
- **explanation**: Each frame has 4 small squares and 1 big square: 5. Two frames give 10, but the shared small square has been counted twice, so 10 − 1 = 9. No other new square is made by the overlap. 10 is tempting because 5 + 5 = 10, but the shared small square is only one square.
- **hint**: Count the squares in each rangoli frame, then check for any square you have counted in both frames.
- **skill**: count_squares_overlapping
- **difficulty**: hard
- **type**: pictorial
- **pictorial**: yes
- **olympiad_section**: achievers

### Q24
- **id**: g4-maths-ch11-b-24
- **stem**: Tushar, Vidya, Waseem and Zubin sit one on each side of a square table, all facing the middle. Tushar faces East. Zubin sits on Tushar's left. Vidya does not face West. Which way does Waseem face?
- **options**:
  - A) North
  - B) East
  - C) West
  - D) South
- **answer**: C
- **explanation**: Tushar faces East, so he sits on the West side. Facing East, his left hand points North, so Zubin sits on the North side and faces South. The South and East sides are left; the person on the East side faces West, and Vidya does not, so Vidya sits on the South side (facing North) and Waseem sits on the East side, facing West. East is tempting because Waseem sits on the East side, but everyone faces the middle, so the person on the East side faces West.
- **hint**: Draw the table with North at the top. Seat Tushar first, then Zubin, and remember each child faces the middle of the table.
- **skill**: table_facing_puzzle
- **difficulty**: hard
- **type**: logical
- **pictorial**: no
- **olympiad_section**: achievers

## Pictorial notes
- Pictorial items: Set A Q01, Q04, Q05, Q06, Q11, Q12, Q14, Q16, Q18, Q20, Q21, Q23; Set B Q04, Q06, Q08, Q11, Q12, Q14, Q16, Q17, Q20, Q21, Q22, Q23.
- Figure kinds: a compass rose, mirror and water-image panels on dot grids (a given drawing beside a dotted mirror line plus four numbered answer figures), a light-bar number display beside a mirror, a town road map and a robot grid with a North arrow, a robot route, two hedge mazes, hidden-shape panels and a copy-the-shape drawing, and line figures for counting triangles, squares and rectangles.
- Picture answers: for the mirror, water-image and hidden-shape items the four answer drawings are numbered 1 to 4 under the panels, and the options read Figure 1 to Figure 4 in that order.
- Exactness: every figure is drawn from the generator's geometry, and the verify script reads it back from the SVG alone: stroke end points on the dot grid (grid spacing taken from the dots), counting-figure line ends, compass arms and the marked arm, road and route points, robot arrow tips, maze hedge lines and gate gaps, and the lit bars of the display.
- Proofs: mirror and water images are computed from the drawn strokes (left-right or top-bottom flip) and compared with all four answer drawings, so exactly one matches. Each given drawing was checked to have no symmetry that would make its image the same as the drawing itself or as any quarter, half or three-quarter turn of it, and the wrong answers include turned and wrongly flipped versions. Triangles, squares and rectangles were counted by brute force over every set of 3 or 4 points where drawn lines meet, with collinear and overlapping lines merged, so each shape is counted once. Hidden shapes were found by trying every position on the grid; mazes were solved by breadth-first search (and all routes up to two steps longer were listed); walks, turns and the seating puzzle were simulated, and every seating clue was checked to be needed.
- Repo rule: no figure carries data-* attributes (the svg-data-attr audit rule from origin/main #82); figures hold only drawing attributes, classes and visible text.
- Scope guard: coding, ranking and alphabet items belong to Chapter 10, and clocks, calendars and combinations to Chapter 12; pattern and analogy formats belong to Chapter 9, and Chapter 6's symmetry-completion and square-with-diagonals counting figures are not reused.
- Every SVG is self-contained (no external fonts, CSS, scripts or images), carries `role="img"` + `aria-label`, and each item also has an `alt` line that describes the drawing without giving the answer and names every printed number. Label text is 10–18 px in the viewBox; scale with `max-width: 100%; height: auto;`.
- olympiad_section values use the house tokens: `logical` = Logical Reasoning, `achievers` = Achievers. The easy and medium items are `logical` and the 5 hard items in each set are `achievers`.
- All keys were computed and asserted by `/workspace/scratch/gen_g4ch11.py` (figures in `/workspace/scratch/g4ch11_figs.py`, geometry and solvers in `/workspace/scratch/g4ch11_logic.py`); `/workspace/scratch/verify_g4ch11.py` re-checks the saved file, reads every figure back from the SVG, recomputes each key, re-runs the uniqueness checks and runs the review rules R1–R6.
- Original content only: written from public topic headings; not affiliated with SOF or any exam board; no past or sample paper items were used or copied, and stems were checked for near-duplicates against the repo's Maths items and Chapters 5–10.
